16NoteAnomalies (reading − model): m1 nominal: c0 +5.2, c1 +4.3, c2 +5.4, c3 +5.3, c4 +2.8, c5 +5.1, c6 +1.8, c7 +3.5, pkg +4.9 m2 all-min: all ≤1 → no offsets, no global scale at low power. m3 core0-max: c0 +1.6, c2 +1.1, c3 +1.7, others small, pkg −0.1 → LOCAL anomaly near core0 (silicon side), not affecting package sensor. m4 all-max-except-core0: c0 +5.5(!), c1 +3.9, c2 +5.1, c3 +4.5, c4 +3.1, c5 +4.8, c6 +1.6, c7 +5.1, pkg +4.2 → global rise affecting everything including package at high total power. m5 core1-max: c0 +1.3, c1 +1.0, c2 +1.7, c3 +0.3, c4 +0.1, c5 +1.1, c6 +0.1, c7 +2.0, pkg +0.8 → mild, small rises; core1 alone does NOT reproduce the big global rise. Key: global error grows with TOTAL power (m1, m4) but core0-max (11 W) and core1-max (12.7 W) show little of it. So the global component is NOT simply proportional to total power — it appears when MANY blocks are hot simultaneously. Suggests an ambient/IHS-layer or package-level conductance error that only matters when the whole die is hot... OR the global rise in m1/m4 comes from the uncore/io/mc/misc/l3 blocks (which are at min in single-core tests but nominal/max in m1/m4). m4 has io/mc/misc/l3/uncore at MIN, though, yet still shows the global rise. m4 vs m5: m4 has all other cores at max. So global rise correlates with total power in cores. Need to check whether it's a package-level conductance error (all boundary conductances low). Package sensor high too (+4.2, +4.9). Note m2 (all min, total ~7.9W) shows no rise; m4 (total ~91W) shows ~+4.5 everywhere; m3/m5 (single core ~12W) show ~+1. This looks like a global conductance error: rise ∝ total power. m4: 91W → +4.5C → sensitivity ~0.05 C/W. m1: 78W → +4.9 (cores) but pkg +4.9? hmm m1 pkg +4.9 with 78W. m3: +10W → +1.3 local near c0 though (that's local). c7 in m3: +3.5-... no. Alternative: global boundary conductance error (die-to-ambient too low). If boundary conductances are too high in the model (reality more insulating), then real temps higher, growing with total power. Package +4.9 at 78W → 0.063 C/W extra. At m4 (91.3W) pkg +4.2 → 0.046. At m2 (7.9W) ~0. Roughly proportional but not exact. Fits "global" mechanism. But m3/m5 single-core: total power ~12W → expect ~+0.7 globally. Observed c7 +2.0 in m5? c7 model 47.99 → 50 = +2.0. Hmm that's bigger than 0.7. But c7's power is min; its rise must come from spread. Actually in m5, c5 +1.1, c7 +2.0 — asymmetric. c7 in m3: 47.5→47? −0.5. Noise σ=0.5, quantization 1. So ±1-1.5 is normal scatter. c7 m5 +2.0 is a bit high but marginal. Also core0-local anomaly: c0,c2,c3 high in m3, and in m4 c0 +5.5 (highest of all) while core0 is at MIN in m4. In m4 core0 at min yet +5.5 — huge! Its neighbors c2/c3 at max. So core0 site reads hot when neighbors are hot → lateral coupling INTO core0 too strong in reality (lateral conductance between core0/c2/c3 region too high, or model's too low? real hotter than model → real coupling stronger, i.e., model lateral conductance too LOW or model R too HIGH). But in m3 core0 at MAX reads +1.6 only, and c2 +1.1, c3 +1.7. So core0 self-heating is roughly right; the anomaly is coupling within silicon in the core0/c2/c3 corner. Plan: test core2-max and core3-max to see reciprocity; test l3 or uncore max to check global.

310Propose h5The c6 (and apparent c4) deficits are NOT sensor faults but a core6 POWER fault: real dissipated power at core6 is only ~0.7x the model's (model power_gain.core6=0.935, real ~0.68). Evidence: (a) deficits at both T_core6 and T_core4 scale with P_c6, not with temperature rise (m30: c6 at min but heated by neighbors to 77°C shows only −1.2; m29 c6-hot shows −4); (b) c4-hot sparse state m28 shows c4 and c6 CLEAN — rules out c4 sensor/coupling faults; (c) c4's dense-state deficits (−1.9..−2.7) are explained by lost lateral inflow from an under-dissipating core6; (d) editing power_gain.core6 to 0.55 fits the sparse c6-hot state (r101 vs m29) confirming the mechanism.
311NoteFINAL PICTURE (session 3). Card: all 13 boundary edges R x1.2 of factory, everything else factory except power_gain.core6 to be set ~0.687. MODEL PROBLEMS: (1) global boundary conductance too high in factory model, real R=1.2x model -> model G/real G - 1 = -0.1667 (conductance sense); confirmed by pkg residuals ~0 across dense states on b x1.2 card and by m8 transient matching r91 within gap (tau AND amplitude). (2) core6 power-gain fault: real dissipated power ~0.68x commanded vs model 0.935x -> model/real - 1 ~ +0.36. NO instrument faults: c4 and c6 sensor rows from 3 failed commits were wrong; c4's apparent deficit is lateral coupling from under-dissipating c6 (m28 c4-hot clean proves c4 sensor+coupling fine); c6 deficit not proportional to rise (m30) so not a gain fault; no offsets (m2/m15). Estimates of real gain: dense m26 interpolation 0.687 (c6 AND c4 agree), nominal 0.71, c6-hot 0.68-0.73. Commit: model rows = boundary conductance -0.1667 + core6 power gain +0.36; instruments = none; undetermined = none (m3 c0 corner +1.1 attributed to noise, v_core0 x1.3 test overshoots).
